(\bm{x}_{t}^{s},\bm{v}_{t}^{s})_{t\geqslant 0}
solve the underdamped Langevin dynamics (
6
) with the initial state
(\bm{x}_{0}^{s},\bm{v}_{0}^{s})
.
Through the chain rule differentiation of
\bm{x}_{t}^{s}
with respect to the parameter
s
, we derive the sensitivity equation

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\bm{x}_{t}^{s}=D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t}^{s}(\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0})+D_{\bm{v}}\bm{x}_{t}^{s}(\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}).

Integration over
s\in[0,1]
provides the error decomposition

\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{t}=\int_{0}^{1}\Big(D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t}^{s}(\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0})+D_{\bm{v}}\bm{x}_{t}^{s}(\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0})\Big)\mathrm{d}s.

(42)

Finally, applying (
41
) and (
42
) generates the contractivity bound:

|\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{t}|\leqslant\sqrt{2}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big)\mathrm{d}s=\sqrt{2}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big),

which completes the proof of Lemma
17
.
∎

While the exponential decay of the first variation process
\bm{W}_{t}
is sufficient for bounding the gradient of
u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
, estimating its Hessian requires analyzing the second-order sensitivity of the underdamped Langevin dynamics (
6
).

A.3
First variation process: Proof of coupling contractivity

We now prove the contractivity of the first variation process
\bm{W}_{t}
with respect to perturbations in the initial state
(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0})
. This property provides the necessary second-order information for analyzing the underdamped Langevin dynamics (
6
).

Lemma 18
.

Under Assumption
with the initial configuration

(\bm{Q}_{0},\bm{P}_{0})=(\bm{I}_{d},\bm{O}_{d})~\text{or}~(\bm{O}_{d},\bm{I}_{d}),

for the first variation processes

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{FirstVariation}\big(\bm{Q}_{0},\bm{P}_{0},\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0},(\bm{B}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}\big),

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{FirstVariation}\big(\bm{Q}_{0},\bm{P}_{0},\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime},\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime},(\bm{B}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}\big),

their discrepancy satisfies the contractive bound

(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})\preccurlyeq\frac{32M_{3}^{2}}{m^{2}}e^{-\frac{mt}{4M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big)^{2}\bm{I}_{d},\quad\forall t\geqslant 0.

Proof.

Building upon the analytical framework established in Lemma
16
, define

\bm{F}_{t}=(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}.

Computing the time derivative of
\bm{F}_{t}
with respect to
t
yields the evolution equation:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bm{F}_{t}

\displaystyle=-(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{A}_{t}^{\prime}\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{A}_{t}\bm{W}_{t})+\text{transpose}

\displaystyle=-(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{A}_{t}(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})-(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{A}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{A}_{t})\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}+\text{transpose}

\displaystyle=-\bm{F}_{t}^{(1)}-\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)},

(43)

with constituent matrices defined as

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{t}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}(\bm{A}_{t}^{\top}\bm{S}+\bm{S}\bm{A}_{t})(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t}),

\displaystyle\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{A}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{A}_{t})\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}+\text{transpose}.

Applying the matrix inequality (
39
) directly to
\bm{F}_{t}^{(1)}
yields the coercive bound

\bm{F}_{t}^{(1)}\succcurlyeq\frac{m}{M_{2}}(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})=\frac{m}{M_{2}}\bm{F}_{t}.

(44)

For
\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}
, we first decompose it using the expressions of
\bm{A}_{t}
and
\bm{A}_{t}^{\prime}
in (
38
),

\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}=(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{Q}_{t}+\bm{P}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{t})^{\top}(\bm{H}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{H}_{t})\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}+\text{transpose}.

Applying the Young’s inequality with parameter
k>0
generates

\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}\succcurlyeq-\|\bm{H}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{H}_{t}\|\bigg(k(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{Q}_{t}+\bm{P}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{t})^{\top}(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{Q}_{t}+\bm{P}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{t})+\frac{1}{k}(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime})^{\top}\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}\bigg),

(45)

where Lemma
17
provides the distance bound of
\bm{H}_{t}
and
\bm{H}_{t}^{\prime}
:

\|\bm{H}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{H}_{t}\|\leqslant\frac{M_{3}}{M_{2}}|\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{t}|\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{2}M_{3}}{M_{2}}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big).

Furthermore, the quadratic term in (
45
) admits the upper bound

\displaystyle(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{Q}_{t}+\bm{P}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{t})^{\top}(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{Q}_{t}+\bm{P}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{P}_{t})

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})^{\top}\bm{S}(\bm{W}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{W}_{t})=\bm{F}_{t}.

and (
40
) implies the inequality

(\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime})^{\top}\bm{Q}_{t}^{\prime}\preccurlyeq 2e^{-\frac{mt}{M_{2}}}\bm{I}_{d}.

Substituting the estimates above into (
45
) yields the estimate of
\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}
:

\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}\succcurlyeq-\frac{\sqrt{2}M_{3}}{M_{2}}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big)\bigg(k\bm{F}_{t}+\frac{2}{k}e^{-\frac{mt}{M_{2}}}\bm{I}_{d}\bigg).

(46)

We synthesize the estimates of
\bm{F}_{t}^{(1)}
and
\bm{F}_{t}^{(2)}
from (
44
) and (
46
) to derive

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bm{F}_{t}\preccurlyeq-\frac{m}{M_{2}}\bm{F}_{t}+\frac{\sqrt{2}M_{3}}{M_{2}}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\delta\bigg(k\bm{F}_{t}+\frac{2}{k}e^{-\frac{mt}{M_{2}}}\bm{I}_{d}\bigg),

(47)

where
\delta=|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|
denotes the initial state discrepancy parameter. Given
\delta>0
, we choose
k
to be moderately small to ensure
\bm{F}_{t}
in (
47
) has a negative coefficient:

\frac{m}{2M_{2}}=\frac{\sqrt{2}M_{3}}{M_{2}}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\delta k~\Longrightarrow~k=\frac{m}{2\sqrt{2}M_{3}\delta}e^{\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}.

Substituting this
k
value into (
47
) yields the refined differential inequality

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bm{F}_{t}\preccurlyeq-\frac{m}{2M_{2}}\bm{F}_{t}+\frac{8(M_{3}\delta)^{2}}{M_{2}m}e^{-\frac{2mt}{M_{2}}}\bm{I}_{d},\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0.

To establish the exponential decay of
\bm{F}_{t}
, we introduce the exponentially weighted matrix
\bm{G}_{t}=e^{\frac{mt}{4M_{2}}}\bm{F}_{t}
, which satisfies the evolution inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bm{G}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{4M_{2}}e^{\frac{mt}{4M_{2}}}\bm{F}_{t}+e^{\frac{mt}{4M_{2}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\bm{F}_{t}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq e^{\frac{mt}{4M_{2}}}\bigg(-\frac{m}{4M_{2}}\bm{F}_{t}+\frac{8(M_{3}\delta)^{2}}{M_{2}m}e^{-\frac{2mt}{M_{2}}}\bm{I}_{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq-\frac{m}{4M_{2}}\bm{G}_{t}+\frac{8(M_{3}\delta)^{2}}{M_{2}m}\bm{I}_{d}.

This Grönwall’s inequality with the initial condition
\bm{G}_{0}=\bm{O}_{d}
generates the upper bound

\bm{G}_{t}\preccurlyeq\frac{32M_{3}^{2}}{m^{2}}\delta^{2}\bm{I}_{d}~\Longrightarrow~\bm{F}_{t}\preccurlyeq\frac{32M_{3}^{2}}{m^{2}}e^{-\frac{mt}{4M_{2}}}\delta^{2}\bm{I}_{d},

thereby completing the proof of Lemma
18
.
∎

Applying Lemma
18
, we can now provide a quantitative estimate of the gradient and Hessian for the Kolmogorov solution
u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
defined in (
11
).

Theorem 19
.

Under Assumptions
and
, the Kolmogorov solution
u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
satisfies

\displaystyle\max\big\{|\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)|,|\nabla_{\bm{v}}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)|\big\}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ce^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}},

\displaystyle\max\big\{\norm{\nabla_{\bm x\bm x}^2 u(\bm x,\bm v,t)},\norm{\nabla_{\bm x\bm v}^2 u(\bm x,\bm v,t)},\norm{\nabla_{\bm v\bm v}^2 u(\bm x,\bm v,t)}\big\}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{m}e^{-\frac{mt}{8M_{2}}},

where the constant
C
depends only on
(M_{i})_{i=1}^{3}
and
(L_{i})_{i=1}^{2}
.

Proof.

First applying Lemma
16
with the decay estimate (
41
), we establish the exponential decay of the first variation processes:

\max\big\{\norm{D_{\bm x}\bm x_t},\norm{D_{\bm v}\bm x_t}\big\}\leqslant\sqrt{2}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}},\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0.

Combining this with the gradient representation
\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0},t)
in (
35
), we deduce

|\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0},t)|\leqslant\mathbb{E}^{(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0})}\big[\norm{D_{\bm x}\bm x_t}|\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t})|\big]\leqslant Ce^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}.

The identical argument applied to the estimate for
\nabla_{\bm{v}}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
,
hence we obtain the first inequality in Theorem
19
.

To establish the Hessian estimates, consider two distinct initial states
(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0})
and
(\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime},\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime})
and define the corresponding solutions
(\bm{x}_{t},\bm{v}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
and
(\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime},\bm{v}_{t}^{\prime})_{t\geqslant 0}
to the underdamped Langevin dynamics (
6
) driven by the same Brownian motion
(\bm{B}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
. Then

\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime},\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime},t)-\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0},t)=\mathbb{E}\Big[(D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime})^{\top}\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime})-(D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t})\Big]=I_{1}+I_{2},

(48)

where the expectation is taken over
(\bm{B}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
, and the constituent terms decompose as

I_{1}=\mathbb{E}\Big[(D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime}-D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime})\Big],\qquad I_{2}=\mathbb{E}\Big[(D_{\bm{x}}\bm{x}_{t})^{\top}(\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime})-\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t}))\Big].

For
I_{1}
estimation, Lemma
18
yields the exponential decay property

\norm{D_{\bm x}\bm x_t'- D_{\bm x}\bm x_t}\leqslant\frac{C}{m}e^{-\frac{mt}{8M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big),

which leads to the inequality

|I_{1}|\leqslant\mathbb{E}\Big[\norm{D_{\bm x}\bm x_t'- D_{\bm x}\bm x_t}|\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime})|\Big]\leqslant\frac{C}{m}e^{-\frac{mt}{8M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big).

(49)

The
I_{2}
term is controlled through Lemma
17
via the chain of inequalities

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\Big[\norm{D_{\bm x} \bm x_t}|\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime})-\nabla f(\bm{x}_{t})|\Big]\leqslant C\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\norm{D_{\bm x}\bm x_t}|\bm{x}_{t}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{t}|\Big]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\cdot e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\cdot e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big)

\displaystyle\leqslant Ce^{-\frac{mt}{M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big).

(50)

Synthesizing the estimates in (
48
)(
49
)(
50
), we establish the composite bound

\big|\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime},\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime},t)-\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0},t)\big|\leqslant\frac{C}{m}e^{-\frac{mt}{8M_{2}}}\big(|\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{x}_{0}|+|\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime}-\bm{v}_{0}|\big).

The arbitrariness of initial states
(\bm{x}_{0},\bm{v}_{0})
and
(\bm{x}_{0}^{\prime},\bm{v}_{0}^{\prime})
implies the Hessian norm control:

\norm{\nabla_{\bm x\bm x}^2 u(\bm x,\bm v,t)}\leqslant\frac{C}{m}e^{-\frac{mt}{8M_{2}}}.

The identical methodology applies to the mixed derivative
\nabla_{\bm{x}\bm{v}}^{2}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
and the velocity Hessian
\nabla_{\bm{v}\bm{v}}^{2}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
, completing the full Hessian estimate.
∎

Theorem
19
directly implies the derivative bound for the discrete Poisson solution

\phi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})=h\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},kh)

under the condition
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
,
concluding the proof of Theorem
1
.

Appendix B
Uniform-in-time moment bound for SG-UBU

For convenience, introduce the notations

\bm{z}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{x}\\
\bm{v}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},\quad\bm{Z}_{k}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}_{k}\\
\bm{V}_{k}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d},\quad\bm{S}=\begin{bmatrix}2\bm{I}_{d}&\bm{I}_{d}\\
\bm{I}_{d}&\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d\times 2d},

then the Lyapunov function
\mathcal{V}(\bm{x},\bm{v})=\big(2|\bm{x}|^{2}+2\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{v}+|\bm{v}|^{2}\big)^{4}
can be compactly written as

\mathcal{V}(\bm{z})=(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{z})^{4}.

(51)

B.1
Lyapunov condition for FG-BU integrator

To begin with, we verify the Lyapunov condition for the FG-UBU integrator.

Lemma 20
.

Under Assumptions
, let the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
; for any
\bm{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{V}\big((\Phi_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}\circ\Phi_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}\big)\bm{z}_{0}\big)\Big]\leqslant\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)\mathcal{V}(\bm{z}_{0})+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}},

where the constant
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

Proof.

From the expressions of the flow solutions
\Phi_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}
and
\Phi_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}
in Section
2.2
, the one-step FG-BU update
\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}=(\bar{\bm{x}}_{1},\bar{\bm{v}}_{1})\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
can be explicitly represented as

\left\{\begin{aligned} \bar{\bm{x}}_{1}&=\bm{x}_{0}+\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{v}_{0}-\frac{h(1-e^{-2h})}{2M_{2}}\nabla U(\bm{x}_{0})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{s},\\
\bar{\bm{v}}_{1}&=e^{-2h}\bm{v}_{0}-\frac{he^{-2h}}{M_{2}}\nabla U(\bm{x}_{0})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{s}.\end{aligned}\right.

Using
\nabla U(\bm{0})=\bm{0}
and the mean value theorem, it holds

\frac{1}{M_{2}}\nabla U(\bm{x}_{0})=\bm{H}\bm{x}_{0},~~~~\text{where}~\bm{H}=\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}U(s\bm{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}s\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}

is positive definite with
\frac{m}{M_{2}}\bm{I}_{d}\preccurlyeq\bm{H}\preccurlyeq\bm{I}_{d}
. Thus the FG-BU update
\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}=(\bar{\bm{x}}_{1},\bar{\bm{v}}_{1})
reads

\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}=\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}+\sqrt{h}\bm{w},

(52)

where we employ the notations

\bm{A}=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{I}_{d}-\dfrac{h(1-e^{-2h})}{2}\bm{H}&\dfrac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{I}_{d}\vskip 6.0pt\\
-he^{-2h}\bm{H}&e^{-2h}\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix},\quad\bm{w}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}h}}\begin{bmatrix}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{h}\dfrac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{s}\\
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{s}\end{bmatrix}.

It is clear that
\bm{w}
is a Gaussian random variable with

\mathbb{E}[\bm{w}]=\bm{0},\qquad\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{w}|^{8}\big]\leqslant Cd^{4},

where the constant
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

Next, we prove that for any step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
, the following matrix inequality holds:

\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{A}\preccurlyeq\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)\bm{S}.

(53)

The inequality (
53
) serves as the coercivity condition for the FG-BU integrator. Direct calculation yields the upper bound of
\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{A}
:

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{A}

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}2\bm{I}_{d}-2h\bm{H}+\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h^{2}\bm{H}^{2}&\bm{I}_{d}-\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}\vskip 6.0pt\\
\bm{I}_{d}-\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}&\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\preccurlyeq\begin{bmatrix}2\bm{I}_{d}-\dfrac{7}{4}h\bm{H}&\bm{I}_{d}-\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}\vskip 6.0pt\\
\bm{I}_{d}-\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}&\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix}.

(using
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
and
\bm{H}\preccurlyeq\bm{I}_{d}
)

Thus using the expression of
\bm{S}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d\times 2d}
, there holds

\displaystyle\bm{S}-\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{A}

\displaystyle\succcurlyeq\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{7}{4}h\bm{H}&\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}\vskip 6.0pt\\
\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}&\dfrac{1-e^{-4h}}{2}\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\succcurlyeq\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{7}{4}h\bm{H}&\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}\vskip 6.0pt\\
\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}h\bm{H}&h\bm{H}\end{bmatrix}

(using
\dfrac{1-e^{-4h}}{2}\geqslant h
and
\bm{I}_{d}\succcurlyeq\bm{H}
)

\displaystyle\succcurlyeq\dfrac{mh}{M_{2}}\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{7}{4}\bm{I}_{d}&\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}\bm{I}_{d}\vskip 6.0pt\\
\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}\bm{I}_{d}&\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix}\succcurlyeq\dfrac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bm{S},

(using
\bm{H}\succcurlyeq\dfrac{m}{M_{2}}I_{d}
)

where the last inequality follows from

\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{7}{4}&\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}\vskip 4.0pt\\
\dfrac{1+e^{-4h}}{2}&1\end{bmatrix}\succcurlyeq\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}2&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}~\Longleftrightarrow~\begin{bmatrix}\dfrac{3}{2}&e^{-4h}\vskip 4.0pt\\
e^{-4h}&1\end{bmatrix}\succcurlyeq 0~\Longleftrightarrow~\frac{3}{2}-e^{-8h}\geqslant 0.

This completes the proof of inequality (
53
).

The positive definite matrix
\bm{S}
admits a unique symmetric square root

\sqrt{\bm{S}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{5}}\begin{bmatrix}3\bm{I}_{d}&\bm{I}_{d}\\
\bm{I}_{d}&2\bm{I}_{d}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d\times 2d}.

This gives the representation
\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{z}=\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{z}\big|^{2}
for
\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
. Using the identity
\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}=\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}+\sqrt{h}\bm{w}
, we obtain the moment bound for the one-step FG-BU update
\bm{z}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{8}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}+\sqrt{h\bm{S}}\bm{w}\big|^{8}

\displaystyle=\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{8}+28\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{6}h\cdot\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{w}\big|^{2}+70\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{4}h^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{w}\big|^{4}

\displaystyle~~~~+28\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{2}h^{3}\cdot\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{w}\big|^{6}+h^{4}\cdot\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{w}\big|^{8}

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{8}+Cdh\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{6}+Cd^{2}h^{2}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{4}+Cd^{3}h^{3}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{2}+Cd^{4}h^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{8}+\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{8}+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}},

where we have utilized the binomial theorem and odd powers of
\bm{w}
vanish in the expansion. Then the contractivity inequality (
53
) yields the moment estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}^{\top}\bm{S}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1})^{4}\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigg(1+\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\big[(\bm{z}_{0}^{\top}\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{A}\bm{z}_{0})^{4}\big]+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigg(1+\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)^{4}(\bm{z}_{0}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{z}_{0})^{4}+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)(\bm{z}_{0}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{z}_{0})^{4}+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}},

concluding proof of the Lyapunov condition for the FG-BU integrator.
∎

Notably, Lemma
20
establishes the Lyapunov condition directly for the FG-UBU integrator. While a corresponding condition can be derived for the continuous underdamped Langevin dynamics (
6
), our analytical framework bypasses its continuous counterpart, operating entirely at the discrete level.

B.2
Lyapunov condition for SG-BU integrator

Based on Lemma
20
and the fact that SG-BU employs an unbiased stochastic gradient, we can conveniently establish the Lyapunov condition for the SG-UBU integrator. This approach also applies to variance-reduced stochastic gradient sampling algorithms, as the unbiasedness property still holds (see Lemmas
25
and
29
).

Proof of Lemma
2
.

Following the proof of Lemma
20
, we denote
\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}=(\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h})\bm{z}_{0}
and
\bm{z}_{1}=(\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\theta))\bm{z}_{0}
as the one-step updates of FG-BU and SG-BU. Then

\left\{\begin{aligned} \bm{x}_{1}&=\bm{x}_{0}+\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{v}_{0}-\frac{h(1-e^{-2h})}{2M_{2}}b(\bm{x}_{0},\theta)+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{s},\\
\bm{v}_{1}&=e^{-2h}\bm{v}_{0}-\frac{he^{-2h}}{M_{2}}b(\bm{x}_{0},\theta)+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{s}.\end{aligned}\right.

Thus we can write the stochastic gradient error as

\bm{x}_{1}-\bar{\bm{x}}_{1}=h\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{x}},~~~~\bm{v}_{1}-\bar{\bm{v}}_{1}=h\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{v}},

where the error terms are given by

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{x}}=-\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2M_{2}}(b(\bm{x}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x}_{0})),~~~~\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{v}}=-\frac{e^{-2h}}{M_{2}}(b(\bm{x}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x}_{0})).

From Assumption
we obtain the unbiased condition and the moment bound

\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{x}}]=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{v}}]=\bm{0},\quad\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{x}}|^{8}\big]\leqslant Cd^{4}h^{8},\quad\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{v}}|^{8}\big]\leqslant Cd^{4}h^{8}.

Define
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}=(\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{x}},\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{v}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
, giving
\bm{z}_{1}=\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}+h\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}
with

\mathbb{E}[\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}]=\bm{0},\quad\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}|^{8}\big]\leqslant Cd^{4}h^{8}.

Exploiting the Lyapunov condition of the FG-BU integrator in Lemma
20
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{z}_{1}\big|^{8}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}+h\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}\big|^{8}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{8}+28h^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big(\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{6}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}\big|^{2}\Big)+70h^{4}\mathbb{E}\Big(\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{4}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}\big|^{4}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+28h^{6}\mathbb{E}\Big(\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{2}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}\big|^{6}\Big)+h^{8}\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}\big|^{8}

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{8}+\frac{mh}{12M_{2}}\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bar{\bm{z}}_{1}\big|^{8}+\frac{Ch}{m^{3}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}|^{8}\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigg(1+\frac{mh}{12M_{2}}\bigg)\bigg(\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{2M_{2}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{8}+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}}\bigg)+\frac{Ch}{m^{3}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}|^{8}\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{3M_{2}}\bigg)\mathbb{E}\big|\sqrt{\bm{S}}\bm{z}_{0}\big|^{8}+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}}.

Recalling the Lyapunov function
\mathcal{V}(\bm{z})=(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{S}\bm{z})^{4}
, the inequality above implies

\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{V}(\bm{z}_{1})\big]\leqslant\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{3M_{2}}\bigg)\mathcal{V}(\bm{z}_{0})+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}},

which completes the proof of Lemma
2
.
∎

We note that the eighth moment bound of the stochastic gradient error
b(\bm{x},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x})
is essential in the proof above, as the eighth moment of
\bm{\Delta}_{\bm{z}}
is required.

B.3
Uniform-in-time moment bound of SG-UBU integrator

Next, it is convenient to derive the uniform-in-time moment bound for the SG-UBU integrator by simply applying an additional
\Phi_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}
flow on the SG-BU integrator.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

For the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
, the following bounds hold for any
\bm{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
:

\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{V}\big(\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h/2}\bm{z}_{0}\big)\big]\leqslant C\mathcal{V}(\bm{z}_{0})+Cd^{4},~~~~\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{V}\big(\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\theta)\bm{z}_{0}\big)\big]\leqslant C\mathcal{V}(\bm{z}_{0})+Cd^{4},

where
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.
These inequalities can be easily found from the explicit expressions of the solution flows
\Phi_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}
and
\Phi_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\theta)
. Then for
\bm{X}_{0}=\bm{0}
and
\bm{V}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},M_{2}^{-1}\bm{I}_{d})
, we have the moment bound

\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{V}\big(\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h/2}\bm{Z}_{0}\big)\big]\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}}.

For
k\geqslant 2
, the SG-UBU solution is written as

\bm{Z}_{k}=\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h/2}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\theta_{k})\circ\bigg(\prod_{l=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\theta_{l})\bigg)\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h/2}\bm{Z}_{0}.

The Lyapunov condition of SG-BU provided in Lemma
2
thus yields

\sup_{k\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{V}(\bm{Z}_{k})\big]\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}},

which completes the proof of Theorem
3
.
∎

Finally, we employ Theorem
3
to show the existence and moment bound for the invariant distribution of SG-UBU.

Proof of Lemma
4
.

Employing the Krylov–Bogolyubov theorem (Theorem 4.21,
32
), when the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
, the Lyapunov condition in Lemma
2
implies that SG-BU has an invariant distribution
\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
satisfying

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\bm{z}|^{8}\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{z})\mathrm{d}\bm{z}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}}.

(54)

Define distribution semigroups for
\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{t}
and
\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{t}(\theta)
:

\displaystyle P_{t}^{\mathfrak{U}}\mu

\displaystyle:=\text{distribution of }\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{t}\bm{z}_{0}~~\text{with}~\bm{z}_{0}\sim\mu,

\displaystyle P_{t}^{\mathfrak{B}}\mu

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Big[\text{distribution of }\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{t}(\theta)\bm{z}_{0}~\text{with}~\bm{z}_{0}\sim\mu\Big].

Then the invariant distribution
\hat{\pi}_{h}
of SG-BU satisfies:

P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}\hat{\pi}_{h}=\hat{\pi}_{h}.

Now define the distribution
\pi_{h}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2d}
as

\pi_{h}=P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}\hat{\pi}_{h}.

This
\pi_{h}
satisfies the same moment bound (
54
), and the identity

\pi_{h}=P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}\hat{\pi}_{h}=P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}\pi_{h},

confirming that
\pi_{h}
is an invariant distribution of SG-UBU.
∎

It should be noted that while Lemma
4
establishes the existence of an invariant distribution, it does not guarantee uniqueness, a property that is not required for the subsequent analysis.

Appendix C
Local error analysis for SG-UBU

This section provides fourth moment estimates for the stochastic gradient error,
\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}
, and the discretization error,
\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}(h)
. The analysis requires comparing the explicit formulas for the one-step evolution of the exact solution, the full gradient update, and the stochastic gradient update, which we present below for clarity.

Exact solution in one-step:
\bm{Z}_{k}(h)

\small\left\{\begin{aligned} \bm{X}_{k}(h)&=\bm{X}_{k}+\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s},\\
\bm{V}_{k}(h)&=e^{-2h}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s}.\end{aligned}\right.

One-step of FG-UBU update:
\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}

\small\left\{\begin{aligned} \bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}&=\bm{X}_{k}+\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{h(1-e^{-h})}{2M_{2}}\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s},\\
\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}&=e^{-2h}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{he^{-h}}{M_{2}}\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s}.\end{aligned}\right.

One-step of SG-UBU update:
\bm{Z}_{k+1}

\small\left\{\begin{aligned} \bm{X}_{k+1}&=\bm{X}_{k}+\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{h(1-e^{-h})}{2M_{2}}b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s},\\
\bm{V}_{k+1}&=e^{-2h}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{he^{-h}}{M_{2}}b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s}.\end{aligned}\right.

These expressions are derived by applying the
\Phi_{t}^{\mathfrak{U}}
and
\Phi_{t}^{\mathfrak{B}}
solution flows in the UBU integrator, and can also be found directly in Example 8 of
54
.
Recall that
\bm{Y}_{k}
is the intermediate position defined in (
9
), and the stochastic gradient indices
(\theta_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
are generated independently from
\mathbb{P}_{\theta}
.

C.1
Estimate of stochastic gradient error

With the explicit expression for the stochastic gradient error established in (
20
), we now prove the corresponding moment bounds in Lemma
5
.

Proof of Lemma
5
.

Under Assumption
, the gradient expression (
20
) gives

\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{X}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}\big|^{4}\leqslant Ch^{8}\mathbb{E}\big|b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\big|^{4}\leqslant C\sigma^{4}d^{2}h^{8},

yielding the position stochastic gradient error bound

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{X}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}\big|^{4}}\leqslant C\sigma^{2}dh^{4}.

Similarly, the velocity error bound follows

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{V}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}\big|^{4}}\leqslant C\sigma^{2}dh^{2},

which completes the proof.
∎

Notably, the position and velocity components of the stochastic gradient error converge at different rates with respect to the step size
h
. This discrepancy in order is a direct consequence of their respective coefficients in the explicit form (
20
).

C.2
Estimate of discretization error

Our analysis of the fourth moment of the discretization error relies on the explicit expressions for the local errors
\delta{\bm{X}}_{k}
and
\delta{\bm{V}}_{k}
provided in Equations (40) and (42) of
54
.
The proof relies on a delicate decomposition of the discretization error using integration by parts.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

Utilizing the explicit expressions of
\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}
and
\bm{Z}_{k}(h)
,
the discretization errors
\delta{\bm{X}}_{k}=\bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}-\bm{X}_{k}(h)
and
\delta{\bm{V}}_{k}=\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}-\bm{V}_{k}(h)
are written as

\displaystyle\delta{\bm{X}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{h(1-e^{-h})}{2M_{2}}\big(\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(h/2))-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\big)+\bm{I}_{6}+\bm{I}_{7},

(55a)

\displaystyle\delta{\bm{V}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{he^{-2h}}{M_{2}}\big(\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(h/2))-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\big)+\bm{I}_{1}+\bm{I}_{2}+\bm{I}_{3}+\bm{I}_{4}+\bm{I}_{5},

(55b)

with the error terms
\bm{I}_{1}
to
\bm{I}_{7}
given by

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{M_{2}}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{M_{2}}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\nabla^{2}U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})^{\top}\nabla^{3}U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{4}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{M_{2}^{2}}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\nabla^{2}U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime},

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{5}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}}}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s^{\prime\prime}},

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{0}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s^{\prime})}}{2}\nabla^{2}U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime}))\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

\displaystyle\bm{I}_{7}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{0}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime})}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime}))\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

For convenience, we define the deviation of
\bm{Y}_{k}
by

\delta{\bm{Y}}_{k}=\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{X}_{k}(h/2)=\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{0}^{h/2}\frac{1-e^{-(h-2s)}}{2}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))\mathrm{d}s,

then its norm
|\delta{\bm{Y}}_{k}|
admits the upper bound

|\delta{\bm{Y}}_{k}|\leqslant Ch\int_{0}^{h/2}|\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))|\mathrm{d}s.

From the uniform-in-time moment bound in Theorem
3
and Hölder’s inequality we derive

\mathbb{E}|\delta{\bm{Y}}_{k}|^{4}\leqslant Ch^{7}\int_{0}^{h/2}\mathbb{E}|\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))|^{4}\mathrm{d}s\leqslant Ch^{7}\int_{0}^{h/2}\mathbb{E}\big(|\bm{X}_{k}(s)|^{2}+d\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{8}}{m^{2}}.

Applying the same approach, we establish fourth moment bounds of
\bm{I}_{1}
to
\bm{I}_{4}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{1}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{9}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}|\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))|^{4}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{12}}{m^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{2}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{9}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}|\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})|^{4}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{12}}{m^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{3}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{9}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}|\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})|^{8}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}h^{12}}{m^{4}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{4}|^{4}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{9}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big|\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\big|^{4}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{12}}{m^{2}}.

For the Gaussian stochastic integral
\bm{I}_{5}
, its second moment is controlled via

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{5}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{2}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathbb{E}\bigg|\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s^{\prime\prime}}\bigg|^{2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{2}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big|\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ch^{2}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\big(|\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime})|^{2}+d\big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{5}}{m},

and thus the fourth moment is bounded by

\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{5}|^{4}=3\Big(\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{5}|^{2}\Big)^{2}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{10}}{m^{2}}.

(56)

Therefore, in the discretization error
\delta{\bm{V}}_{k}=\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}-\bm{V}_{k}(h)
, we select the martingale component
\delta_{m}{\bm{V}}_{k}=\bm{I}_{5}
with the high-order term
\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}
as

\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}=\frac{he^{-2h}}{M_{2}}\big(\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(h/2))-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\big)+\bm{I}_{1}+\bm{I}_{2}+\bm{I}_{3}+\bm{I}_{4}.

The fourth moment of
\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}
then satisfies

\mathbb{E}|\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}|^{4}\leqslant Ch^{4}\mathbb{E}|\delta{\bm{Y}}_{k}|^{4}+C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{i}|^{4}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}h^{12}}{m^{4}}.

(57)

Finally, we bound the fourth moment of
\bm{I}_{6}
and
\bm{I}_{7}
. Observing the inequality

|\bm{I}_{6}|\leqslant Ch\int_{0}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}|\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

then Hölder’s inequality yields the moment bound

\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{6}|^{4}\leqslant Ch^{8}\int_{0}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathbb{E}|\bm{V}_{k}(s^{\prime})|^{4}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{12}}{m^{2}}.

Next, from the chain rule

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\Big(e^{2s}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))\Big)=e^{2s}\Big(2\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))+\nabla^{2}U(\bm{X}_{k}(s))\bm{V}_{k}(s)\Big),

we deduce the equality

\bm{I}_{7}=-\frac{1}{M_{2}}\int_{h/2}^{h}\mathrm{d}s\int_{h/2}^{s}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\int_{h-s^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}}\Big(e^{-2(h-s^{\prime\prime})}\nabla U(\bm{X}_{k}(s^{\prime\prime}))\Big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}=-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{I}_{1}+\bm{I}_{2}).

Hence the fourth moment of
\bm{I}_{7}
is bounded by

\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{7}|^{4}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{12}}{m^{2}}.

In total, the moment bound in the position variable reads

\mathbb{E}|\delta{\bm{X}}_{k}|^{4}\leqslant C\big(\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{6}|^{4}+\mathbb{E}|\bm{I}_{7}|^{4}\big)\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{12}}{m^{2}}.

(58)

Inequalities (
56
)–(
58
) complete the proof of Lemma
6
.
∎

We note that the estimate for the term
\bm{I}_{3}
in the preceding proof requires both the bound on the third derivative,
\nabla^{3}U(\bm{x})
, and the uniform eighth moment bound of the numerical solution
(\bm{X}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
. For this reason, Assumption
requires an eighth moment bound on the stochastic gradient deviation
b(\bm{x},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x})
.

C.3
Estimate of long-time displacement

At the
K
-th step, the state displacement
\bm{Z}_{K}-\bm{Z}_{0}
can be estimated as follows.

Lemma 21
.

Under Assumptions
and
, let the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
, and the initial state

\bm{Z}_{0}=(\bm{X}_{0},\bm{V}_{0})\text{~with~}\bm{X}_{0}=\bm{0}\text{~and~}\bm{V}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},M_{2}^{-1}\bm{I}_{d});

then the SG-UBU solution
(\bm{X}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
satisfies

\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{K}-\bm{Z}_{0}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{CdKh}{m},

where the constant
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

Proof.

We write the one-step SG-UBU update
\bm{Z}_{k+1}
as

\displaystyle\bm{X}_{k+1}-\bm{X}_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{h(1-e^{-h})}{2M_{2}}b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})+\sqrt{h}\bm{M}_{k},

(59a)

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{k+1}-\bm{V}_{k}

\displaystyle=-(1-e^{-2h})\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{he^{-h}}{M_{2}}b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})+\sqrt{h}\bm{N}_{k},

(59b)

where the martingales
\bm{M}_{k}
and
\bm{N}_{k}
are defined as

\bm{M}_{k}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}h}}\int_{0}^{h}\frac{1-e^{-2(h-s)}}{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s},~~~~\bm{N}_{k}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{M_{2}h}}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-2(h-s)}\mathrm{d}\bm{B}_{kh+s},

and their second moments satisfy

\mathbb{E}|\bm{M}_{k}|^{2}\leqslant Cd,~~~~\mathbb{E}|\bm{N}_{k}|^{2}\leqslant Cd.

Applying Cauchy’s inequality to (
59
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg|\bm{X}_{K}-\bm{X}_{0}-\sqrt{h}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bm{M}_{k}\bigg|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\bigg|\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\big(\bm{X}_{k+1}-\bm{X}_{k}-\sqrt{h}\bm{M}_{k}\big)\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant K\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\bigg|\frac{1-e^{-2h}}{2}\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{h(1-e^{-h})}{2M_{2}}b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})\bigg|^{2}\leqslant\frac{CdK^{2}h^{2}}{m},

and similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg|\bm{V}_{K}-\bm{V}_{0}-\sqrt{h}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bm{N}_{k}\bigg|^{2}=\mathbb{E}\bigg|\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\big(\bm{V}_{k+1}-\bm{V}_{k}-\sqrt{h}\bm{N}_{k}\big)\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant K\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}\bigg|-(1-e^{-2h})\bm{V}_{k}-\frac{he^{-h}}{M_{2}}b(\bm{Y}_{k},\theta_{k})\bigg|^{2}\leqslant\frac{CdK^{2}h^{2}}{m}.

Since
(\bm{M}_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
is a martingale, we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\bm{X}_{K}-\bm{X}_{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2h\mathbb{E}\bigg|\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bm{M}_{k}\bigg|^{2}+\frac{CdK^{2}h^{2}}{m}

\displaystyle=2h\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}|\bm{M}_{k}|^{2}+\frac{CdK^{2}h^{2}}{m}\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m}(Kh+K^{2}h^{2}).

Similarly, for
\bm{V}_{k}-\bm{V}_{0}
we have the same inequality

\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{K}-\bm{Z}_{0}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m}(Kh+K^{2}h^{2}).

(60)

On the other hand, from the uniform-in-time moments in Theorem
3
, we have

\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{K}-\bm{Z}_{0}|^{2}\leqslant 2\mathbb{E}\big(|\bm{Z}_{K}|^{2}+|\bm{Z}_{0}|^{2}\big)\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m}.

(61)

Integrating (
60
), (
61
), and the algebraic inequality
\min\{x+x^{2},1\}\leqslant 2x
we obtain

\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{K}-\bm{Z}_{0}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{CdKh}{m},

which completes the proof of Lemma
21
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs for MSE and numerical bias of SG-UBU

Recall that the time average error of SG-UBU is expressed as in (
18
):

\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}f(\bm{X}_{k})-\pi(f)=\frac{\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0})-\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{K})}{Kh}+\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(R_{k}+S_{k}+T_{k}),

where the error terms
R_{k}
,
S_{k}
, and
T_{k}
are given by

\displaystyle R_{k}=\frac{\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k+1})-\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})}{h},\qquad S_{k}=\frac{\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})-\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k}(h))}{h},

\displaystyle T_{k}=\frac{\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k}(h))-\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k})}{h}+f(\bm{X}_{k})-\pi(f).

D.1
Mean square error of SG-UBU

Proof of Theorem
7
.

According to (
18
), we only need to estimate the second moments:

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}R_{k}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg],\quad\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}S_{k}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg],\quad\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}T_{k}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg].

First, the stochastic gradient error
R_{k}
can be written as

R_{k}=\frac{1}{h}\big(\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k+1})-\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})\big)=\frac{1}{h}(\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\phi_{h}\big(\lambda\bm{Z}_{k+1}+(1-\lambda)\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}\big)\mathrm{d}\lambda,

we decompose
R_{k}=R_{k,1}+R_{k,2}
, where
R_{k,1}
is a martigale and
R_{k,2}
is a high-order remainder:

\displaystyle R_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}(\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})^{\top}\nabla\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}),

\displaystyle R_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}(\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\nabla\phi_{h}\big(\lambda\bm{Z}_{k+1}+(1-\lambda)\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}\big)-\nabla\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1})\Big)\mathrm{d}\lambda.

The bounds of
\nabla\phi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
and
\nabla^{2}\phi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
in
Theorem
1
imply the inequalities

|R_{k,1}|\leqslant\frac{C}{mh}|\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}|,\qquad|R_{k,2}|\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{2}h}|\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}|^{2}.

Hence utilizing the stochastic gradient error estimate in Lemma
5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{k,1}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{2}h^{2}}\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}d}{m^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{k,2}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{4}h^{2}}\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}|^{4}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{4}d^{2}h^{2}}{m^{4}}.

Since
R_{k,1}
is a martingale with
\mathbb{E}[R_{k,1}|\bm{Z}_{k}]=0
,

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}R_{k,1}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}[R_{k,1}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}dK}{m^{2}}.

On the other hand, by Cauchy’s inequality

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}R_{k,2}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant K\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}[R_{k,2}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{4}d^{2}K^{2}h^{2}}{m^{4}}.

Combining the estimates of
\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}R_{k,1}
and
\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}R_{k,2}
yields

\frac{1}{K^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}R_{k}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{m^{2}K}+\frac{\sigma^{4}d^{2}h^{2}}{m^{4}}\bigg).

(62)

The discretization error
S_{k}
analysis parallels the approach for
R_{k}
. Adopting the analytical framework in Lemma
6
, we decompose the local error
\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}(h)
as

\bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}-\bm{X}_{k}(h)=\delta{\bm{X}}_{k},~~~~\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}-\bm{V}_{k}(h)=\delta_{m}{\bm{V}}_{k}+\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k},

where
\delta_{m}{\bm{V}}_{k}
is a martingale and
\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}
is a higher-order term. Now
S_{k}
decomposes as

\displaystyle S_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\Big(\delta{\bm{X}}_{k}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k})+\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{v}}\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k})\Big),

\displaystyle S_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\delta_{m}{\bm{V}}_{k}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{v}}\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k}),

\displaystyle S_{k,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}(h))^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\nabla\phi_{h}\big(\lambda\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}+(1-\lambda)\bm{Z}_{k}(h)\big)-\nabla\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k})\Big)\mathrm{d}\lambda.

Theorem
1
yields the bounds of
S_{k,1}
and
S_{k,2}
:

|S_{k,1}|\leqslant\frac{C}{mh}\big(|\delta{\bm{X}}_{k}|+|\delta_{h}{\bm{V}}_{k}|\big),\qquad|S_{k,2}|\leqslant\frac{C}{mh}|\delta_{m}{\bm{V}}_{k}|.

Hence leveraging the discretization error estimates in Lemma
6
produces

\mathbb{E}[S_{k,1}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{4}}{m^{4}},\qquad\mathbb{E}[S_{k,2}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{3}}{m^{3}}.

(63)

The martingale property of
S_{k,2}
gives

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}S_{k,2}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}[S_{k,2}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{CdKh^{3}}{m^{3}}.

(64)

By Cauchy’s inequality,
\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}S_{k,1}
has the moment bound

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}S_{k,1}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant K\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathbb{E}[S_{k,1}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}K^{2}h^{4}}{m^{4}}.

(65)

Exploiting the expressions of
\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}
and
\bm{Z}_{k}(h)
in Appendix
C
, it is easy to derive

\mathbb{E}|\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{4}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{2}}{m^{2}},~~~~\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{k}(h)-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{4}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{2}}{m^{2}}.

Applying Theorem
1
we obtain

|S_{k,3}|\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{2}h}\sqrt{|\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}(h)|^{2}\big(|\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{2}+|\bm{Z}_{k}(h)-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{2}\big)},

and thus by the discretization error moment estimate in Lemma
6
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[S_{k,3}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{4}h^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}(h)|^{4}\mathbb{E}\big(|\bar{\bm{Z}}_{k+1}-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{4}+|\bm{Z}_{k}(h)-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{4}\big)}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{4}h^{2}}\bigg(\frac{d^{2}h^{6}}{m^{2}}+\frac{dh^{5}}{m}\bigg)\frac{dh}{m}=C\bigg(\frac{d^{2}h^{4}}{m^{6}}+\frac{d^{3}h^{5}}{m^{7}}\bigg).

(66)

Then by Cauchy’s inequality:

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}S_{k,3}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{d^{2}K^{2}h^{4}}{m^{6}}+\frac{d^{3}K^{2}h^{5}}{m^{7}}\bigg).

(67)

Synthesizing the inequalities (
64
), (
65
), and (
67
) gives

\frac{1}{K^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}S_{k}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{dh^{3}}{m^{3}K}+\frac{d^{2}h^{4}}{m^{6}}+\frac{d^{3}h^{5}}{m^{7}}\bigg).

(68)

Finally, decompose the martingale
T_{k}
as
T_{k}=T_{k,1}+T_{k,2}
with

T_{k,1}=\frac{1}{h}\big(\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k}(h))-\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{k})\big),\qquad T_{k,2}=f(\bm{X}_{k})-\pi(f).

Applying Theorem
1
produces the bound

|T_{k,1}|\leqslant\frac{C}{mh}|\bm{Z}_{k}(h)-\bm{Z}_{k}|~\Longrightarrow~\mathbb{E}[T_{k,1}^{2}]\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{2}h^{2}}\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{k}(h)-\bm{Z}_{k}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m^{3}h}.

For
T_{k,2}
, take
\bm{Z}_{*}\sim\pi
independent of
\bm{X}_{k}
, then
\mathbb{E}|\bm{X}_{*}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m}
and

\mathbb{E}[T_{k,2}^{2}]\leqslant\mathbb{E}|f(\bm{X}_{k})-f(\bm{X}_{*})|^{2}\leqslant C\mathbb{E}\big(|\bm{X}_{k}|^{2}+|\bm{Z}_{*}|^{2}\big)\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m}.

Combining these moment estimates, we derive

\mathbb{E}[T_{k}^{2}]\leqslant C\big(\mathbb{E}[T_{k,1}^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[T_{k,2}^{2}]\big)\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{d}{m^{3}h}+\frac{d}{m}\bigg)\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m^{3}h}.

Leveraging the martingale property of
T_{k}
, we establish the time-averaged moment bound

\frac{1}{K^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}T_{k}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m^{3}Kh}.

(69)

Combining the estimates (
62
), (
68
), and (
69
), we derive

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(R_{k}+S_{k}+T_{k})\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{d}{m^{3}Kh}+\frac{\sigma^{4}d^{2}h^{2}}{m^{4}}+\frac{d^{2}h^{4}}{m^{6}}+\frac{d^{3}h^{5}}{m^{7}}\bigg).

(70)

On the other hand, Lemma
21
provides the displacement moment estimate:

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0})-\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{K})}{Kh}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant\frac{C}{m^{2}K^{2}h^{2}}\mathbb{E}|\bm{Z}_{0}-\bm{Z}_{K}|^{2}\leqslant\frac{Cd}{m^{3}Kh}.

(71)

Integrating (
70
), (
71
) with the time average error expression (
18
) yields

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}f(\bm{X}_{k})-\pi(f)\bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{d}{m^{3}Kh}+\frac{\sigma^{4}d^{2}h^{2}}{m^{4}}+\frac{d^{2}h^{4}}{m^{6}}+\frac{d^{3}h^{5}}{m^{7}}\bigg),

completing the proof of Theorem
7
.
∎

The MSE analysis for mini-batch SG-UBU requires a quantitative bound on the high-order moments of the stochastic gradient. The following lemma establishes the key estimate.

Lemma 22
.

Let
x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be vectors with bounded eighth moments:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|x_{i}|^{8}\leqslant 1.

Denote
\bar{x}_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
\theta
be a random subset of
\{1,\dots,N\}
of size
p
. Then

\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}x_{i}-\bar{x}_{N}\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]\leqslant\frac{C}{p^{4}},

where the constant
C
is absolute.

The proof mainly relies on Hoeffding’s inequality
(
33
)
and Rosenthal’s inequality
(
51
;
19
)
, which are stated below.

Theorem 23

(Hoeffding)
.

Let
\Pi=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\}
be a finite set. Let
(X_{1},\dots,X_{p})
be a sample drawn without replacement from
\Pi
, and let
(Y_{1},\dots,Y_{p})
be a sample drawn with replacement from
\Pi
. If
f(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})
is a continuous convex function on the sample space,

\mathbb{E}[f(X_{1},\dots,X_{p})]\leqslant\mathbb{E}[f(Y_{1},\dots,Y_{p})].

Theorem 24

(Rosenthal)
.

Let
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{p}
be independent mean-zero random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
k\geqslant 2
, there exists a constant
C_{k}
depending only on
k
such that

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{p}Z_{j}\bigg|^{k}\Bigg]\leqslant C_{k}\max\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\big[|Z_{j}|^{k}\big],\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\big[|Z_{j}|^{2}\big]\bigg)^{\frac{k}{2}}\Bigg).

Proof of Lemma
22
.

WLOG assume
\bar{x}_{N}=0
.
Let
X_{1},\cdots,X_{p}
denote independent samples from
\{1,\cdots,N\}
(with replacement), then by Hoeffding’s inequality we have

\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}x_{i}\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}X_{j}\bigg|^{8}\Bigg].

(72)

Since
X_{1},\cdots,X_{p}
are independent, applying Rosenthal’s inequality with
k=8
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{p}X_{j}\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\max\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{j}|^{8}\big],\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{j}|^{2}\big]\bigg)^{4}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\bigg(p\,\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{1}|^{8}\big]+p^{4}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{1}|^{2}\big]\Big)^{4}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant Cp^{4}\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{1}|^{8}\big]=\frac{Cp^{4}}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|x_{i}|^{8}\leqslant Cp^{4}.

Hence we derive the upper bound

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}X_{j}\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]\leqslant\frac{C}{p^{4}}.

(73)

Combining the estimates (
72
) and (
73
) yields the result of Lemma
22
.
∎

By Lemma
22
, Assumption
implies the following eighth moment control of the mini-batch gradient error: for any
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and random subset
\theta\subset\{1,\dots,N\}
with size
p
,

\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x})-\nabla U(\bm{x})\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]\Bigg)^{\tfrac{1}{4}}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}d}{p}.

Hence the stochastic gradient in mini-batch SG-UBU satisfies all items of Assumption
with
\sigma^{2}
replaced by
C\sigma^{2}/p
. Therefore, Theorem
8
follows directly from Theorem
7
.

D.2
Numerical bias of SG-UBU

Proof of Theorem
9
.

To streamline the notation, expectations are taken with respect to the numerical invariant distribution
\pi_{h}
and the stochastic gradient index
\theta
, which we omit in the proof. From Lemma
4
,
\bm{Z}_{0}=(\bm{X}_{0},\bm{V}_{0})
drawn from
\pi_{h}
satisfies the moment bound:

\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{Z}_{0}|^{8}\big]\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}}.

According to (
24
),
the numerical bias is the sum of the expected stochastic gradient error,
\mathbb{E}[R_{0}]
, and the expected discretization error,
\mathbb{E}[S_{0}]
, where

R_{0}=\frac{\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{1})-\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}_{1}})}{h},\qquad S_{0}=\frac{\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})-\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0}(h))}{h}.

We begin with the stochastic error term
\mathbb{E}[R_{0}]
. A Taylor expansion of
\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{1})
around
\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}
using the mean value theorem gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\phi_{h}\big(\lambda\bm{Z}_{1}+(1-\lambda)\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}\big)\mathrm{d}\lambda\bigg].

The key insight is that the first-order term of this expansion vanishes in expectation due to the martingale property of the stochastic gradient error established in (
21
):

\mathbb{E}\big[(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})^{\top}\nabla\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})\big]=0.

This simplification leaves only the second-order terms:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{0}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\nabla\phi_{h}\big(\lambda\bm{Z}_{1}+(1-\lambda)\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}\big)-\nabla\phi_{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})\Big)\mathrm{d}\lambda\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\lambda\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}\phi_{h}\big(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}+\lambda\varphi(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})\big)\mathrm{d}\varphi\bigg)\mathrm{d}\lambda\,(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}_{1}})\bigg].

Recall that the components of the local error
\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}
converge at different rates. Lemma
5
provides the moment bounds:

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{X}_{1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{1}\big|^{4}}\leqslant C\sigma^{2}dh^{4},\qquad\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{V}_{1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{1}\big|^{4}}\leqslant C\sigma^{2}dh^{2}.

Since the position error
\bm{X}_{1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{1}
is of a higher order in
h
than the velocity error, its contribution to the bias is smaller. Using the Hessian bound
\|\nabla^{2}\phi_{h}\|\leqslant C/m^{2}
from Theorem
1
, we can bound these higher-order terms:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\bm{X}_{1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\lambda\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{x}\bm{v}}^{2}\phi_{h}\big(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}+\lambda\varphi(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})\big)\mathrm{d}\varphi\bigg)\mathrm{d}\lambda\,(\bm{V}_{1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{1})\bigg]\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\bm{X}_{1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{1})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\lambda\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{\bm{x}\bm{x}}^{2}\phi_{h}\big(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}+\lambda\varphi(\bm{Z}_{1}-\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1})\big)\mathrm{d}\varphi\bigg)\mathrm{d}\lambda\,(\bm{X}_{1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{1})\bigg]\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{3}}{m^{2}}.

The dominant contribution to the bias therefore comes from the velocity error component. Using the definition of
\bm{M}_{0}
from the theorem statement, we arrive at the estimate:

\bigg|\mathbb{E}[R_{0}]-\frac{1}{2h}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\bm{V}_{1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{1})^{\top}\bm{M}_{0}(\bm{V}_{1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{1})\Big]\bigg|\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{2}}.

(74)

Finally, we substitute the explicit expression for the velocity error from (
20
):

\bm{V}_{1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{1}=-\frac{he^{-h}}{M_{2}}\big(b(\bm{Y}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{0})\big).

This substitution into (
74
), along with the linear approximation
e^{-h}=1+\mathcal{O}(h)
, yields the leading-order term for the stochastic error’s contribution to the bias:

\bigg|\mathbb{E}[R_{0}]-\frac{h}{2M_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b(\bm{Y}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{0})\big)^{\top}\bm{M}_{0}\big(b(\bm{Y}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{0})\big)\Big]\bigg|\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{2}}.

For the discretization error component,
\mathbb{E}[S_{0}]
, we directly follow the decomposition in Theorem
7
and write
S_{0}=S_{0,1}+S_{0,2}+S_{0,3}
, where

\displaystyle S_{0,1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\Big(\delta\bm{X}_{0}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0})+\delta_{h}\bm{V}_{0}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{v}}\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0})\Big),

\displaystyle S_{0,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}\delta_{m}\bm{V}_{0}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{v}}\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0}),

\displaystyle S_{0,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h}(\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}-\bm{Z}_{0}(h))^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\nabla\phi_{h}\big(\lambda\bar{\bm{Z}}_{1}+(1-\lambda)\bm{Z}_{0}(h)\big)-\nabla\phi_{h}(\bm{Z}_{0})\Big)\mathrm{d}\lambda.

The martingale property of
\delta_{m}\bm{V}_{0}
stated in Lemma
6
implies
\mathbb{E}[S_{0,2}]=0
, and thus the expectation simplifies to
\mathbb{E}[S_{0}]=\mathbb{E}[S_{0,1}]+\mathbb{E}[S_{0,3}]
. Utilizing the moment estimates of
S_{0,1}
and
S_{0,3}
in (
63
) and (
66
), and the step size constraint
h\leqslant\frac{m}{d}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}|S_{0,1}|\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{2}},\qquad\mathbb{E}|S_{0,3}|\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{3}}.

Combining these estimates provides a bound on the expected total local error:

\big|\mathbb{E}[S_{0}]\big|\leqslant\mathbb{E}|S_{0,1}|+\mathbb{E}|S_{0,3}|\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{3}}.

(75)

Synthesizing the estimates for the stochastic error in (
74
) and the discretization error in (
75
), we arrive at a complete characterization of the numerical bias:

\bigg|\pi_{h}(f)-\pi(f)-\frac{h}{2M_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(b(\bm{Y}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{0})\big)^{\top}\bm{M}_{0}\big(b(\bm{Y}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{0})\big)\Big]\bigg|\leqslant\frac{Cdh^{2}}{m^{3}}.

This expression isolates the leading-order term of the bias, which is proportional to the variance of the stochastic gradient. By applying the established bounds
\|\bm{M}_{0}\|\leqslant C/m^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}\big[|b(\bm{Y}_{0},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{0})|^{2}\big]\leqslant C\sigma^{2}d
, we can bound this leading term, confirming that the overall numerical bias exhibits first-order convergence:

|\pi_{h}(f)-\pi(f)|\leqslant C\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}dh}{m^{2}}+\frac{dh^{2}}{m^{3}}\bigg).

This completes the proof of Theorem
9
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10
.

We begin by recalling the continuous Poisson equation in (
13
):

\bm{v}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})-\bigg(\frac{1}{M_{2}}\nabla U(\bm{x})+2\bm{v}\bigg)^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{v}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})+\frac{2}{M_{2}}\Delta_{\bm{v}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})=\pi(f)-f(\bm{x}).

(76)

Theorem
19
provides an exponential decay estimate for the Kolmogorov solution
u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)
:

\max\big\{|\nabla_{\bm{x}}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)|,|\nabla_{\bm{v}}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)|\big\}\leqslant CL_{1}e^{-\frac{mt}{2M_{2}}}.

By integrating this inequality with respect to
t
over
(0,\infty)
and using the integral representation
\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})=\int_{0}^{\infty}u(\bm{x},\bm{v},t)\mathrm{d}t
, we obtain uniform bounds on the derivatives of
\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
:

\max\big\{|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})|,|\nabla_{\bm{v}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})|\big\}\leqslant\frac{CL_{1}}{m}.

With these derivative bounds established, we can rearrange the Poisson equation (
76
) to isolate the Laplacian term. Since the potential gradient
\nabla U(\bm{x})
and the test function
f(\bm{x})
both exhibit at most linear growth in
|\bm{x}|
, all terms in the equation other than the Laplacian grow at most linearly in
|\bm{x}|
and
|\bm{v}|
. This allows us to establish a growth bound on the Laplacian of the velocity component of
\phi_{0}
:

|\Delta_{\bm{v}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})|\leqslant\frac{C}{m}\sqrt{|\bm{x}|^{2}+|\bm{v}|^{2}+d},\qquad\forall\bm{x},\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(77)

To complete the proof, we need to bound the second moment of the Hessian of the Poisson solution, averaged over the equilibrium velocity distribution. Thus we define the quantity
\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})
as this velocity-averaged integral:

\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})=\bigg(\frac{M_{2}}{2\pi}\bigg)^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big\|\nabla_{\bm{v}\bm{v}}^{2}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\big\|^{2}e^{-\frac{M_{2}}{2}|\bm{v}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}.

The goal is to show that the expectation of
\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})
with respect to the target distribution
\pi(\bm{x})
is appropriately bounded:

\mathbb{E}^{\pi}\big[\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})\big]\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{3}}.

(78)

Our strategy is to leverage local elliptic regularity estimates. We partition the velocity space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
into unit hypercubes centered at integer vectors
\bm{j}=(j_{1},\cdots,j_{d})\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
:

\displaystyle B_{\bm{j}}

\displaystyle=\Big(j_{1}-\frac{1}{2},j_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\Big)\times\cdots\times\Big(j_{d}-\frac{1}{2},j_{d}+\frac{1}{2}\Big),

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{\bm{j}}

\displaystyle=(j_{1}-1,j_{1}+1)\times\cdots\times(j_{d}-1,j_{d}+1).

Standard local
L^{2}
-estimates for elliptic equations allow us to bound the integral of the Hessian over a block
B_{\bm{j}}
by the integral of the Laplacian and the function itself over the larger block
\tilde{B}_{\bm{j}}
:

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\bm{j}}}\big\|\nabla_{\bm{v}\bm{v}}^{2}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\big\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{d}\int_{\tilde{B}_{\bm{j}}}\Big(|\Delta_{\bm{v}}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})|^{2}+|\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})|^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}\int_{\tilde{B}_{\bm{j}}}(|\bm{x}|^{2}+|\bm{v}|^{2}+d)\mathrm{d}\bm{v},

where we have used the growth bound (
77
) for the Laplacian and a similar bound for the solution
\phi_{0}
.

For any
\bm{v}\in\tilde{B}_{\bm{j}}
, its squared norm is related to the squared norm of the center point
\bm{j}
. This relationship allows us to bound the Gaussian weight within each block. For
\bm{v}\in B_{\bm{j}}
, we can bound the exponential term from below in terms of
|\bm{j}|^{2}
, which simplifies the integral. Combining these estimates, we can bound the contribution to
\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})
from each block
B_{\bm{j}}
:

\displaystyle\quad\bigg(\frac{M_{2}}{2\pi}\bigg)^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{B_{\bm{j}}}\big\|\nabla_{\bm{v}\bm{v}}^{2}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\big\|^{2}e^{-\frac{M_{2}}{2}|\bm{v}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{d}\exp\bigg(-\frac{M_2}4\sum_{i=1}^dj_i^2\bigg)\int_{B_{\bm{j}}}\big\|\nabla_{\bm{v}\bm{v}}^{2}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\big\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{M_2}4\sum_{i=1}^dj_i^2\bigg)\int_{\tilde{B}_{\bm{j}}}(|\bm{x}|^{2}+|\bm{v}|^{2}+d)\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{M_2}4\sum_{i=1}^dj_i^2\bigg)\bigg(|\bm{x}|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}j_{i}^{2}+C_{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{M_2}8\sum_{i=1}^dj_i^2\bigg)(|\bm{x}|^{2}+1).

Summing over all blocks
\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
yields a bound on
\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})
. The sum over the exponential terms is a convergent geometric series, resulting in an upper bound for
\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})
that exhibits at most quadratic growth in
|\bm{x}|
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\bigg(\frac{M_{2}}{2\pi}\bigg)^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{B_{\bm{j}}}\big\|\nabla_{\bm{v}\bm{v}}^{2}\phi_{0}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\big\|^{2}e^{-\frac{M_{2}}{2}|\bm{v}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}(|\bm{x}|^{2}+1)\sum_{\bm{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\exp\bigg(-\frac{M_2}8\sum_{i=1}^dj_i^2\bigg)\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}(|\bm{x}|^{2}+1).

Finally, taking the expectation of this inequality with respect to
\pi(\bm{x})
yields the desired result. Since the potential is strongly convex,
\pi(\bm{x})
has finite second moments, giving:

\mathbb{E}^{\pi}\big[\mathcal{Q}(\bm{x})\big]\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}^{\pi}[|\bm{x}|^{2}+1]\leqslant\frac{C_{d}}{m^{3}}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
10
.
∎

The idea of using hypercubes to derive the
L^{2}
-estimate comes from Xu’an Dou.

Appendix E
Proofs for MSE and numerical bias of SVRG-UBU

The analysis of SVRG-UBU follows the same structure as that for mini-batch SG-UBU, establishing stability through uniform-in-time moment bounds and consistency via local error estimates. The discrete Poisson equation is then applied to derive the MSE and numerical bias. A critical distinction, however, is that the SVRG-UBU solution
(\bm{X}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
is non-Markovian. To address this, we define the invariant distribution
\pi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
with respect to the embedded Markovian subsequence
(\bm{X}_{kN/p},\bm{V}_{kN/p})_{k\geqslant 0}
. This subsequence is constructed from the anchor positions, which are updated at the end of each epoch.

Throughout this section, we let
q=N/p
denote the number of inner iterations in each epoch. To formulate the SVRG-UBU integrator compactly, we define a solution flow for the variance-reduced gradient update, analogous to
\Phi_{t}^{\mathfrak{B}}
and
\Phi_{t}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\theta)
. For a given anchor position
\bm{x}_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, this flow is defined as

\Phi_{t}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x}_{*},\theta):\left\{\begin{aligned} \bm{x}_{t}&=\bm{x}_{0},\\
\bm{v}_{t}&=\bm{v}_{0}-\frac{t}{M_{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x}_{0})-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x}_{*})+\nabla U(\bm{x}_{*})\bigg).\end{aligned}\right.

Then SVRG-UBU from Algorithm
3
can be expressed as a single update rule. Since the anchor position is recomputed every
q
steps, the integrator takes the form:

\bm{Z}_{k+1}=\big(\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h/2}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\bm{Y}_{q[k/q]},\theta_{k})\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h/2}\big)\bm{Z}_{k},\qquad k=0,1,\cdots,

(79)

where
\bm{Y}_{k}
is the position component of
\Phi_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}\bm{Z}_{k}
. The term
\bm{Y}_{q[k/q]}
represents the anchor position established at the beginning of the current epoch, which remains fixed for
q
iterations. The random subsets
(\theta_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
of size
p
are drawn independently at each step.

E.1
Uniform-in-time moment bound of SVRG-UBU

Our first step is to establish a uniform-in-time moment bound for the SVRG-UBU solution, which is a prerequisite for proving the existence of an invariant distribution for its embedded Markovian subsequence. Following the analytical approach used for SG-UBU, we begin by analyzing the corresponding BU-type integrator, SVRG-BU. Its update rule is given by

\bm{Z}_{k+1}=\big(\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\bm{X}_{q[k/q]},\theta_{k})\big)\bm{Z}_{k},\qquad k=0,1,\cdots.

(80)

The analysis of SVRG-BU closely parallels that of the standard SG-BU integrator. By directly adapting the proof of Lemma
2
, we establish an analogous Lyapunov condition for SVRG-BU, as stated below.

Lemma 25
.

Under Assumptions
and
, let the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
; for any initial state
\bm{z}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
and the anchor position
\bm{x}_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{V}\big((\Phi^{\mathfrak{U}}_{h}\circ\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{h}(\bm{x}_{*},\theta)\big)\bm{z}_{0})\Big]\leqslant\bigg(1-\frac{mh}{3M_{2}}\bigg)\mathcal{V}(\bm{z}_{0})+\frac{Cd^{4}h}{m^{3}},

where
\theta
is a random subset of
\{1,\cdots,N\}
of size
p
, and
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof proceeds by verifying that the stochastic gradient
b(\bm{x},\bm{x}_{*},\theta)
satisfies the conditions of Assumption
. This allows for the application of Lemma
2
directly.

First, the unbiasedness property,
\mathbb{E}_{\theta}[b(\bm{x},\bm{x}_{*},\theta)]=\nabla U(\bm{x})
, is readily verified from the definition of the estimator. To check the eighth moment condition in Assumption
, we apply the triangle inequality:

|b(\bm{x},\bm{x}_{*},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x})|\leqslant\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x})-\nabla U(\bm{x})\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x}_{*})-\nabla U(\bm{x}_{*})\bigg|.

Computing the eighth moment of both sides yields

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\big[|b(\bm{x},\bm{x}_{*},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x})|^{8}\big]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\,\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x})-\nabla U(\bm{x})\bigg|^{8}+\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x}_{*})-\nabla U(\bm{x}_{*})\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x})-\nabla U(\bm{x})|^{8}+\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\nabla U_{i}(\bm{x}_{*})-\nabla U(\bm{x}_{*})|^{8}\leqslant C(\sigma^{2}d)^{4},

where we have applied Hölder’s inequality. This exactly produces Assumption

\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\big[|b(\bm{x},\bm{x}_{*},\theta)-\nabla U(\bm{x})|^{8}\big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\leqslant C\sigma^{2}d

for arbitrary anchor position
\bm{x}_{*}
.
Therefore, Lemma
2
establishes the Lyapunov condition of the SVRG-BU integrator, regardless of the choice of anchor positions.
∎

The Lyapunov condition established for SVRG-BU in Lemma
25
provides the foundation for bounding the moments of the SVRG-UBU solution. Since the SVRG-UBU integrator differs from its BU-type counterpart only by the stable
\Phi_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}
mapping, their uniform-in-time moment bounds are equivalent. This leads directly to the following theorem.

Theorem 26
.

Under Assumptions
and
, let the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
, and

\bm{Z}_{0}=(\bm{X}_{0},\bm{V}_{0})\text{~with~}\bm{X}_{0}=\bm{0}\text{~and~}\bm{V}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},M_{2}^{-1}\bm{I}_{d});

then the SVRG-UBU solution
(\bm{X}_{k},\bm{V}_{k})_{k=0}^{\infty}
satisfies

\sup_{k\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{Z}_{k}|^{8}\big]\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}},

where the constant
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

By applying the same techniques from the proof of Lemma
4
, we can prove the existence of and establish a moment bound for the invariant distribution of SVRG-UBU’s embedded Markovian subsequence. This subsequence,
(\bm{X}_{kq},\bm{V}_{kq})_{k=0}^{\infty}
, is formed by the states at the end of each epoch (recall that the anchor position is updated every
q=N/p
iterations). The result is described in the following lemma.

Lemma 27
.

Under Assumptions
and
, let the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
; the SVRG-UBU subsequence
(\bm{X}_{kq},\bm{V}_{kq})_{k=0}^{\infty}
has an invariant distribution
\pi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
in
\mathbb{R}^{2d}
, which satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\bm{z}|^{8}\pi_{h}(\bm{z})\mathrm{d}\bm{z}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}},

where the constant
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

Proof.

To formalize the analysis, we first define a semigroup for the variance-reduced gradient update that explicitly depends on a fixed anchor position
\bm{x}_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Analogous to the previous definitions, we have

P_{t}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x}_{*})\mu:=\mathbb{E}_{\theta}\big[\text{distribution of }\Phi^{\mathfrak{B}}_{t}(\bm{x}_{*},\theta)\bm{z}_{0}\text{ with }\bm{z}_{0}\sim\mu\big].

The Lyapunov condition established in Lemma
25
, combined with the Krylov–Bogolyubov theorem, guarantees that the SVRG-BU subsequence
(\bm{X}_{kq},\bm{V}_{kq})_{k=0}^{\infty}
admits an invariant distribution
\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
in
\mathbb{R}^{2d}
. This distribution satisfies the moment bound:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}|\bm{z}|^{8}\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{z})\mathrm{d}\bm{z}\leqslant\frac{Cd^{4}}{m^{4}}.

(81)

By definition of the SVRG-BU integrator, an epoch consists of
q=N/p
inner iterations where the anchor point is fixed. The invariance of
\hat{\pi}_{h}
across these epochs is expressed by the relation

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\Big(\big(P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x})\big)^{q}\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{v}}\Big)\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}=\hat{\pi}_{h}.

(82)

Here, the integral signifies an expectation over the invariant distribution
\hat{\pi}_{h}
. For each state
(\bm{x},\bm{v})
drawn from
\hat{\pi}_{h}
, its position component
\bm{x}
becomes the anchor for an entire epoch of
q
steps. The use of the Dirac measure
\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{v}}
indicates that this epoch starts from the state
(\bm{x},\bm{v})
itself, ensuring the anchor position remains fixed for all subsequent
\mathfrak{B}
steps.

Next, we construct the invariant distribution for the SVRG-UBU subsequence,
\pi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
, using the invariant distribution of the SVRG-BU subsequence,
\hat{\pi}_{h}
. We define
\pi_{h}
by evolving
\hat{\pi}_{h}
through one epoch of SVRG-BU, followed by an additional half-step of the
\mathfrak{U}
flow:

\pi_{h}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\Big(P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}\big(P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x})\big)^{q}\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{v}}\Big)\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}.

(83)

Then
\pi_{h}
inherits the same moment bound given in (
81
).

By applying the semigroup operator
P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}
to both sides of (
83
), we get

\displaystyle P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}\pi_{h}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\Big(P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}\big(P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x})\big)^{q}\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{v}}\Big)\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\Big(\big(P_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x})\big)^{q}\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{v}}\Big)\hat{\pi}_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}=\hat{\pi}_{h}.

Therefore (
83
) can be equivalently written as

\pi_{h}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\Big(\big(P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x})P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}\big)^{q-1}\big(P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}P_{h}^{\mathfrak{B}}(\bm{x})\big)\delta_{\bm{x},\bm{v}}\Big)(P_{h/2}^{\mathfrak{U}}\pi_{h})(\bm{x},\bm{v})\mathrm{d}\bm{x}\mathrm{d}\bm{v}

showing
\pi_{h}
is invariant for the SVRG-UBU subsequence
(\bm{X}_{kq},\bm{V}_{kq})_{k=0}^{\infty}
.
∎

E.2
Local error analysis of SVRG-UBU

We now turn to the local error analysis for the SVRG-UBU integrator. The discretization error is identical to that for SG-UBU, as this term is independent of the gradient estimator. Our focus therefore shifts to bounding the stochastic gradient error, which is where the effect of the variance reduction mechanism becomes apparent. The following lemma, analogous to Lemma
5
, establishes the corresponding fourth moment bounds.

Lemma 28
.

Under Assumptions
and
, let the step size
h\leqslant\frac{1}{4}
, and

\bm{Z}_{0}=(\bm{X}_{0},\bm{V}_{0})\text{~with~}\bm{X}_{0}=\bm{0}\text{~and~}\bm{V}_{0}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},M_{2}^{-1}\bm{I}_{d});

then the stochastic gradient error of SVRG-UBU satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geqslant 0}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{X}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}\big|^{4}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}dh^{4}}{p}\min\bigg\{1,\frac{N^{2}h^{2}}{mp^{2}}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geqslant 0}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{V}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}\big|^{4}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}dh^{2}}{p}\min\bigg\{1,\frac{N^{2}h^{2}}{mp^{2}}\bigg\},

where the constant
C
depends only on
M_{1},M_{2}
.

Proof.

Analogous to the expression for SG-UBU, the stochastic gradient error components of the SVRG-UBU integrator are given by:

\bm{X}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{X}}_{k+1}=-\frac{h(1-e^{-h})}{2M_{2}}\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k}),\qquad\bm{V}_{k+1}-\bar{\bm{V}}_{k+1}=-\frac{he^{-h}}{M_{2}}\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k}),

(84)

where
\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the deviation of the SVRG estimator from the true gradient
\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})
. According to (
29
), this deviation is explicitly written as

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta_{k}}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{Y}_{k})-\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta_{k}}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{Y}_{*})+\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{*})\bigg)-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})

\displaystyle=(\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*})^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta_{k}}\nabla^{2}U_{i}-\nabla^{2}U\bigg)\big(\lambda\bm{Y}_{k}+(1-\lambda)\bm{Y}_{*}\big)\mathrm{d}\lambda,

(85)

where
\bm{Y}_{*}=\bm{Y}_{q[k/q]}
is the anchor position.

To bound the fourth moment of
\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})
, we leverage its integral representation. For a fixed
\lambda\in[0,1]
, we define a set of vectors:

a_{i}(\lambda)=(\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*})^{\top}\big(\nabla^{2}U_{i}-\nabla^{2}U\big)\big(\lambda\bm{Y}_{k}+(1-\lambda)\bm{Y}_{*}\big),\qquad i=1,\cdots,N.

This sequence of vectors has a zero mean, namely,
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i}(\lambda)=0
. We can bound average eighth moment of
a_{i}(\lambda)
using Assumption
:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|a_{i}(\lambda)|^{8}\leqslant|\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*}|^{8}\cdot\frac{C}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\big\|(\nabla^{2}U_{i}-\nabla^{2}U)(\lambda\bm{Y}_{k}+(1-\lambda)\bm{Y}_{*})\big\|^{8}\leqslant C\sigma^{8}|\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*}|^{8}.

This confirms that the conditions of Lemma
22
are met. Applying the lemma to the mini-batch average of these vectors gives:

\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{k}}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta_{k}}a_{i}(\lambda)\bigg|^{8}\Bigg]\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{8}}{p^{4}}|\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*}|^{8}.

(86)

By integrating over
\lambda\in[0,1]
and applying the Jensen’s inequality, we obtain a bound on the eighth moment of
\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})
, which implies a bound on its fourth moment:

\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{k}}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{8}\big]\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{8}}{p^{4}}|\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*}|^{8}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{k}}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{4}\big]\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{4}}{p^{2}}|\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{*}|^{4}.

(87)

Taking the full expectation over the numerical solution yields:

\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{4}\big]\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{4}}{p^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{Y}_{k}-\bm{Y}_{q[k/q]}\big|^{4}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{4}q^{3}}{p^{2}}\sum_{s=q[k/q]}^{k-1}\mathbb{E}|\bm{Y}_{s+1}-\bm{Y}_{s}|^{4}.

(88)

The single-step deviation can be bounded using SVRG-UBU from Algorithm
3
, which implies
(\bm{Y}_{s+1},\bm{V}_{s+1}^{(1)})=\Phi_{h}^{\mathfrak{U}}(\bm{Y}_{s},\bm{V}_{s}^{(2)})
. The uniform-in-time moment bounds then give

\mathbb{E}|\bm{Y}_{s+1}-\bm{Y}_{s}|^{4}\leqslant Ch^{4}\big(\mathbb{E}|\bm{Y}_{s}|^{4}+\mathbb{E}|\bm{V}_{s}^{(2)}|^{4}+d^{2}\big)\leqslant\frac{Cd^{2}h^{4}}{m^{2}}.

Substituting this into (
88
) and noting that the sum has at most
q
terms, we arrive at the final bound for the stochastic gradient deviation:

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{4}\big]}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}dq^{2}h^{2}}{mp}.

(89)

Alternatively, a simpler bound on the stochastic gradient deviation
\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})
that is independent of the distance between the sampling point
\bm{Y}_{k}
and the anchor point
\bm{Y}_{*}
can be obtained via the triangle inequality:

\displaystyle~~~~\mathbb{E}_{\theta_{k}}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{8}\big]\leqslant C\Bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta_{k}}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{Y}_{k})-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\bigg|^{8}+\bigg|\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\theta_{k}}\nabla U_{i}(\bm{Y}_{*})-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\bigg|^{8}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{p^{4}}\bigg(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\big|\nabla U_{i}(\bm{Y}_{k})-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{k})\big|^{8}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\big|\nabla U_{i}(\bm{Y}_{*})-\nabla U(\bm{Y}_{*})\big|^{8}\bigg)\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{8}d^{4}}{p^{4}}.

Then we arrive at a uniform bound on the fourth moment:

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{4}\big]}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}d}{p}.

(90)

Combining this uniform bound with the distance-dependent bound from (
89
) yields a tighter composite estimate using the
\min
function:

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[|\bm{g}_{k}(\theta_{k})|^{4}\big]}\leqslant\frac{C\sigma^{2}d}{p}\min\bigg\{1,\frac{q^{2}h^{2}}{m}\bigg\}=\frac{C\sigma^{2}d}{p}\min\bigg\{1,\frac{N^{2}h^{2}}{mp^{2}}\bigg\}.

(91)

Finally, substituting the comprehensive bound from (
91
) into the local error expressions in (
85
) yields the result and completes the proof.
∎

E.3
MSE of SVRG-UBU

The uniform-in-time moment bound from Theorem
26
and the local stochastic gradient error estimate from Lemma
28
provide the necessary stability and consistency results for the SVRG-UBU integrator. Consequently, the MSE bound in Theorem
11
follows directly by applying the analytical procedure detailed in the proof of Theorem
7
.

E.4
Numerical bias of SVRG-UBU

Proof of Theorem
12
.

Let
q=N/p
be the length of one epoch, and let
\pi_{h}(\bm{x},\bm{v})
be the invariant distribution of the SVRG-UBU subsequence
(\bm{X}_{kq},\bm{V}_{kq})_{k=0}^{\infty}
. Let the initial state
\bm{Z}_{0}\sim\pi_{h}
